<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company quickly squashed the rumor about layoffs.  公司迅速压下了关于裁员的谣言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squelch</td><td>/skweltʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压制，镇压（运动）</td><td>squelch the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops moved in quickly to squelch the rebellion in the province.  军队迅速开进，镇压该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subdue</td><td>/səbˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使顺从；征服</td><td>subdue the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government forces moved south to subdue the rebellion within a week.  政府军南下，一周内镇压了叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suppress</td><td>/səˈpres/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用暴力终止，镇压；封锁，删除</td><td>suppress a rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime used the army to suppress a rebellion in the capital.  该政权动用军队镇压首都的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bridle</td><td>/ˈbraɪdl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>限制</td><td>bridle his anger<br>克制他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bridled her temper and answered the rude question politely.  她克制住脾气，礼貌地回答了那个无礼的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curb</td><td>/ˈkɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>控制，约束</td><td>curb government spending<br>控制政府开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city introduced new rules to curb traffic downtown.  市政府出台新规以限制市区交通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muffle</td><td>/ˈmʌfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压低声音，包裹使消声</td><td>muffle the sound<br>把声音压低</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick curtains muffle the sound of traffic outside the window.  厚窗帘减弱了窗外的车流声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smother</td><td>/ˈsmʌðər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制（表达、说出），压制</td><td>smother a laugh<br>忍住不笑出声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned away to smother a laugh at her odd remark.  他转过身去，忍住对她那句怪话的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stifle</td><td>/ˈstaɪfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制（声音、呼吸等）；阻止，扼杀</td><td>stifle a yawn<br>忍住哈欠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She tried to stifle a yawn while the speaker droned on.  演讲者絮絮不休，她努力忍住哈欠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strangle</td><td>/ˈstræŋɡəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扼制，压制；勒死</td><td>strangle dissent<br>压制异议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy regulation strangled the small firms within two years.  繁重的管制两年内就压垮了这些小公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swallow</td><td>/ˈswɑːloʊ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吞下，咽下；燕子</td><td>swallow the pill<br>吞下药片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He swallowed the bitter medicine without a word of complaint.  他一言不发地咽下了苦药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foment</td><td>/foʊˈment/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>助长，煽动</td><td>foment unrest<br>煽动动乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlets were designed to foment rebellion in the provinces.  这些小册子意在煽动各省叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 instigate</td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>instigate a rebellion<br>煽动叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several officers were accused of helping to instigate the rebellion in the province.  几名军官被指控参与煽动该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stir</td><td>/ˈstɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；搅拌</td><td>stir up anger<br>激起怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article stirred up anger among local residents.  这篇文章激起了当地居民的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 express</td><td>/ɪkˈspres/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>表达，表现；快速的</td><td>express deep gratitude<br>表达深切感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to express the gratitude she felt toward her mentor.  她难以表达对导师的感激之情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 take out</td><td>/teɪk ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>除掉，消灭；取出</td><td>take out the guard<br>干掉那名守卫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single missile took out the bridge before dawn.  黎明前一枚导弹就摧毁了那座桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unleash</td><td>/ənˈliːʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>释放，发动</td><td>unleash a storm of protest<br>引发一场抗议风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision unleashed criticism from every corner of the press.  这一决定引发了媒体各方的批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vent</td><td>/ˈvent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>排放，发泄；通风口</td><td>vent one's frustration<br>发泄挫败感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She vented her frustration in a long letter she never mailed.  她在一封从未寄出的长信里发泄了挫败感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reprieve</b></td><td>/rɪˈpriːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>对…暂缓处刑，免罪</td><td>reprieve the condemned man<br>暂缓处决那名死囚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The governor reprieved the prisoner only hours before the execution.  州长在行刑前几小时才暂缓了对囚犯的处决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absolve</td><td>/əbˈzɑːlv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使无罪，解除责任</td><td>absolve someone of blame<br>免除某人的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report absolved the pilot of all blame.  报告免除了飞行员的全部责任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquit</td><td>/əˈkwɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣判无罪</td><td>acquit of murder<br>宣判谋杀罪不成立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jury acquitted him of all charges.  陪审团宣判他所有罪名不成立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amnesty</td><td>/ˈæmnəsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>大赦，特赦</td><td>grant an amnesty<br>予以大赦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A general amnesty released hundreds of political prisoners.  一次大赦释放了数百名政治犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condone</td><td>/kənˈdoʊn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宽恕；忽视</td><td>condone the violence<br>宽恕这种暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school will never condone violence between its students.  学校绝不会宽恕学生之间的暴力行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excuse</td><td>/ɪkˈskjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>为…辩解，宽恕；借口</td><td>excuse his rudeness<br>为他的粗鲁辩解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing can excuse such careless handling of the evidence.  如此草率地处理证据是无法辩解的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forgive</td><td>/fərˈɡɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宽容，原谅</td><td>forgive a small mistake<br>原谅小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She forgave him quickly, though the insult had stung for days.  她很快原谅了他，尽管那侮辱刺痛了她几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pardon</td><td>/ˈpɑːrdən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赦免，宽恕；原谅</td><td>grant him a pardon<br>给予他赦免</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The president pardoned two men convicted decades earlier.  总统赦免了两名几十年前被定罪的男子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remit</td><td>/rɪˈmɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>减轻，免除；汇款</td><td>remit a penalty<br>免除罚款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge agreed to remit part of the heavy fine.  法官同意减免部分高额罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 respite</td><td>/ˈrespɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>间歇，休息</td><td>a brief respite<br>短暂的休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The afternoon rain gave the firefighters a brief respite from the intense heat.  午后的雨让消防员从酷热中得到短暂的休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spare</td><td>/ˈsper/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>宽恕，免除；备用的</td><td>spare his life<br>饶他一命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The victors spared everyone who surrendered before nightfall.  胜利者宽恕了所有在天黑前投降的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penalize</td><td>/ˈpenəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>处罚，惩罚</td><td>penalize late payment<br>对逾期付款处罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The league penalized the club for breaking transfer rules.  联盟因该俱乐部违反转会规则而对其处罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 punish</td><td>/ˈpʌnɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惩罚，处罚</td><td>punish the offender<br>惩罚违法者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers no longer punish children by making them stand outside.  老师们不再用罚站门外的方式惩罚孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reproach</b></td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 admonish</td><td>/ədˈmɑːnɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>告诫，警告；责备</td><td>admonish someone for lying<br>因撒谎而责备某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher admonished the boys for shouting in the hallway.  老师责备男孩们在走廊里大喊大叫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 berate</td><td>/bɪˈreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉指责，斥责</td><td>berate him publicly<br>公开斥责他</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castigate</td><td>/ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈（公开）指责</td><td>castigate for negligence<br>因疏忽而受斥责</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chide</td><td>/tʃaɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>责备</td><td>chide someone gently<br>温和地责备某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chided her son for forgetting his grandmother's birthday.  她责备儿子忘了祖母的生日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condemn</td><td>/kənˈdem/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责（…为不道德的、邪恶的）</td><td>condemn the attack<br>谴责这次袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 World leaders quickly condemned the attack on the hospital.  各国领导人迅速谴责了对医院的袭击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denounce</td><td>/dɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开指责</td><td>denounce corruption<br>公开指责腐败</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rebuke</td><td>/rɪˈbjuːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>斥责，责难</td><td>a sharp rebuke<br>严厉的斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge rebuked the lawyer for interrupting the witness.  法官斥责律师打断证人发言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprove</td><td>/rɪˈpruːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>温和地责备，警告</td><td>reprove a student mildly<br>温和地批评学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach reproved the player for arguing with the referee.  教练责备那名球员与裁判争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 upbraid</del></td><td>/ʌpˈbreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（严厉地）谴责，责骂</td><td>upbraid him for lying<br>因撒谎而责骂他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager upbraided him for missing three deadlines in one month.  经理因他一个月内三次误期而严厉责骂他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commend</td><td>/kəˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬，表扬</td><td>commend her courage<br>赞扬她的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The principal commended her courage in front of the whole school.  校长在全校面前赞扬了她的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reprobate</b></td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brute</td><td>/ˈbruːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>残暴之人，野蛮人</td><td>a drunken brute<br>醉酒的野蛮之人</td></tr>

<tbody>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiend</td><td>/ˈfiːnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶魔，残忍之人；狂热者</td><td>a cruel fiend<br>残忍的恶魔</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miscreant</td><td>/ˈmɪskriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍，罪犯；邪恶的</td><td>punish the miscreants<br>惩罚这些恶棍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge promised to punish the miscreants who had vandalized the village school.  法官承诺惩罚那些破坏村校的恶棍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rapscallion</td><td>/ræpˈskæliən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流氓，无赖；小捣蛋鬼</td><td>a young rapscallion<br>一个年轻流氓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That little rapscallion stole apples from the neighbor's tree again.  那个小捣蛋鬼又偷了邻居树上的苹果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rascal</td><td>/ˈræskəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，流氓；小淘气</td><td>a little rascal<br>小淘气鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That rascal took the last piece of cake again.  那个小淘气又拿走了最后一块蛋糕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 villain</td><td>/ˈvɪlən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍；反派人物</td><td>the villain of the story<br>故事里的反派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every good film needs a villain the audience loves hating.  每部好片都需要一个让观众爱恨的反派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debased</td><td>/dəˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，贬损的</td><td>debased taste<br>低劣的品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He complained about the debased state of public debate.  他抱怨公共辩论的堕落状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauched</td><td>/dɪˈbɔtʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，堕落的</td><td>a debauched lifestyle<br>放荡的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel follows a debauched young lord toward his ruin.  小说讲一位放荡的年轻贵族走向毁灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degraded</td><td>/dɪˈɡreɪdəd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，卑下的；退化的</td><td>a degraded lifestyle<br>堕落的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel depicts a once proud family in degraded circumstances.  小说描写了一个曾经显赫的家族沦落的境况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depraved</td><td>/diˈpreɪvd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，道德败坏的</td><td>a depraved appetite<br>堕落的嗜好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the film depraved, which only sold more tickets.  批评者称这部电影堕落，结果票卖得更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissolute</td><td>/ˈdɪsəluːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，无节制的</td><td>a dissolute lifestyle<br>放荡的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel follows a dissolute lifestyle of late nights and squandered fortunes.  小说描写了通宵纵乐、挥霍家财的放荡生活方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 libertine</td><td>/ˈlɪbərtiːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡不羁者</td><td>an unrepentant libertine<br>不知悔改的放荡者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play portrays an unrepentant libertine who ruins everyone around him.  这部戏刻画了一个毁掉身边所有人的不知悔改的放荡者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverse</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反常固执的，任性的</td><td>take perverse pleasure<br>反常地感到快意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He takes perverse pleasure in arguing against every popular opinion.  他反常地乐于反驳每一个流行观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverted</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扭曲的，反常堕落的</td><td>a perverted sense of justice<br>扭曲的正义感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He justified the theft with a perverted sense of fairness.  他用一种扭曲的公平感为盗窃辩解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rakish</td><td>/ˈreɪkɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，行为不检点的</td><td>a rakish reputation<br>放荡不检的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His rakish reputation kept respectable families from inviting him to dinner.  他放荡不检的名声使正派家庭不愿请他吃饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 saint</td><td>/ˈseɪnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>圣人，品德高尚的人</td><td>the patience of a saint<br>圣人般的耐心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teaching that class demands the patience of a saint.  教那个班需要圣人般的耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reproof</b></td><td>/rɪˈpruːf/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>a gentle reproof<br>温和的责备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single glance from her served as a silent reproof.  她的一个眼神就成了无声的责备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 commination</del></td><td>/ˌkɑməˈneɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉谴责，诅咒</td><td>a fierce commination<br>一顿严厉的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sermon ended in a commination against greedy landlords.  他的布道以对贪婪地主的严厉谴责收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condemnation</td><td>/ˌkɑːndemˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，指责</td><td>widespread condemnation<br>广泛的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The raid drew swift condemnation from neighboring states.  这次袭击迅速招致邻国的谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denunciation</td><td>/dɪˌnʌnsiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，公开指责</td><td>a public denunciation<br>公开的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech was a fierce denunciation of the new policy.  她的演讲是对新政策的猛烈谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excoriation</td><td>/ɪkˌskɔːriˈeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉指责，痛斥</td><td>public excoriation<br>公开的痛斥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editorial was an excoriation of the mayor's housing policy.  这篇社论是对市长住房政策的痛斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rebuke</td><td>/rɪˈbjuːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>斥责，责难</td><td>a sharp rebuke<br>严厉的斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge rebuked the lawyer for interrupting the witness.  法官斥责律师打断证人发言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stricture</td><td>/ˈstrɪktʃər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>责难，批评</td><td>harsh strictures on<br>对…的严厉批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review contained harsh strictures on the author's careless research.  评论对作者草率的研究提出严厉批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commendation</td><td>/ˌkɑːmənˈdeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>表扬，嘉奖</td><td>receive a commendation for bravery<br>因勇敢获得嘉奖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer received a commendation for rescuing the trapped driver.  这名警官因救出被困司机而获嘉奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reprove</b></td><td>/rɪˈpruːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>温和地责备，警告</td><td>reprove a student mildly<br>温和地批评学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach reproved the player for arguing with the referee.  教练责备那名球员与裁判争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 admonish</td><td>/ədˈmɑːnɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>告诫，警告；责备</td><td>admonish someone for lying<br>因撒谎而责备某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher admonished the boys for shouting in the hallway.  老师责备男孩们在走廊里大喊大叫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blame</td><td>/ˈbleɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>责备，归咎于</td><td>blame someone for something<br>因某事责怪某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't blame me for the broken window.  别把窗户破了怪在我头上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chide</td><td>/tʃaɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>责备</td><td>chide someone gently<br>温和地责备某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chided her son for forgetting his grandmother's birthday.  她责备儿子忘了祖母的生日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 criticize</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪˌsaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>批评，指责；评论</td><td>criticize the decision<br>批评这个决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several members criticized the plan for ignoring small farmers.  几名成员批评该计划忽视了小农。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>repudiate</b></td><td>/rɪˈpjuːdieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，拒绝接受</td><td>repudiate a claim<br>否认一项主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author repudiated the views quoted in the article.  作者否认了文章中引用的观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contradict</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，反驳；与…相矛盾</td><td>contradict the evidence<br>与证据相矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness's second statement flatly contradicted everything she had said earlier.  证人的第二份陈述与她此前所说完全矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disaffirm</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈfɜrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，否定；废除</td><td>disaffirm the contract<br>否认合同效力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court may disaffirm a contract signed by a minor.  法院可以否定未成年人签署的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disavow</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈvaʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拒绝承认，否认</td><td>disavow any knowledge<br>否认知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator moved quickly to disavow any knowledge of the secret payments.  参议员迅速表态，否认知晓那些秘密付款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disclaim</td><td>/dɪsˈkleɪm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，拒绝承认</td><td>disclaim responsibility<br>否认责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company quickly disclaimed any link to the failed venture.  公司迅速否认与那个失败项目有任何关联。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disconfirm</del></td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈfɜrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证明为假，否证</td><td>disconfirm the hypothesis<br>否证该假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New data disconfirmed the theory proposed a decade earlier.  新数据否证了十年前提出的理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disown</td><td>/dɪˈsoʊn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，拒绝承认</td><td>disown his own words<br>否认自己说过的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party quickly disowned the candidate's offensive remarks.  该党迅速否认与该候选人冒犯性言论有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gainsay</td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认</td><td>gainsay the evidence<br>否认证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could gainsay the evidence the inspector had collected.  没有人能否认检查员收集到的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negate</td><td>/nɪˈɡeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；否定，取消</td><td>negate the effect<br>抵消效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless error can negate weeks of careful work.  一个粗心的错误能抹掉数周的细致工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negative</td><td>/ˈneɡətɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>否决，反驳；负面的</td><td>negative the proposal<br>否决这项提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee negatived the motion after a short debate.  委员会经短暂辩论后否决了该动议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refute</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驳倒，反驳</td><td>refute the claim<br>驳斥这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scientist used new data to refute the claim about rising temperatures.  这位科学家用新数据反驳了关于气温上升的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reject</td><td>/rɪˈdʒekt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，驳回；废品</td><td>reject the proposal<br>否决提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee voted to reject the proposal without further discussion.  委员会投票否决了该提案，不再讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acknowledge</td><td>/ækˈnɑːlɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认；对…表示感谢</td><td>acknowledge the mistake<br>承认错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor finally acknowledged that the report contained serious errors.  市长最终承认那份报告有严重错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 admit</td><td>/ədˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认；准许进入</td><td>admit the mistake<br>承认错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally admitted that she had made a serious mistake.  她终于承认自己犯了一个严重的错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allow</td><td>/əˈlaʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>允许，准许</td><td>allow visitors inside<br>允许访客进入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library allows visitors to photograph the manuscripts without flash.  图书馆允许访客不开闪光灯拍摄手稿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avow</td><td>/əˈvaʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开承认，坦率声明</td><td>avow one's beliefs openly<br>公开表明信念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He avowed his support for the strike in front of reporters.  他在记者面前公开表明支持这次罢工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concede</td><td>/kənˈsiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认</td><td>concede the point<br>承认这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally conceded that her opponent had made a fair point.  她最终承认对手说得有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confirm</td><td>/kənˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，确认，进一步确定</td><td>confirm the findings<br>证实这些发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Later tests confirmed what the first team had suspected.  后来的检测证实了第一个团队的猜测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>repugnant</b></td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>morally repugnant behavior<br>道德上可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most readers found the cruelty of that character morally repugnant and unforgivable.  多数读者觉得那个角色的残忍在道德上令人厌恶，不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abhorrent</td><td>/æbˈhɔːrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>abhorrent to most people<br>令多数人厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The practice is abhorrent to anyone with basic decency.  这种做法令任何有基本良知的人厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abominable</td><td>/əˈbɑːmənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，极糟的</td><td>abominable behavior<br>可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His abominable behavior at dinner shocked the guests.  他晚餐时可憎的举止让客人震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awful</td><td>/ˈɑːfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，可怕的</td><td>an awful smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An awful smell came from the forgotten lunchbox.  被遗忘的饭盒里飘出难闻的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgusting</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a disgusting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was left in a disgusting state after the party.  聚会后厨房脏得让人恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distasteful</td><td>/dɪˈsteɪstfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的，讨厌的</td><td>a distasteful joke<br>令人不快的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found the whole discussion deeply distasteful.  她觉得整场讨论令人极不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loathsome</td><td>/ˈloʊðsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>a loathsome smell<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loathsome smell rose from the neglected drain.  无人清理的下水道升起令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noisome</td><td>/ˈnɔɪsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的；令人极厌恶的</td><td>a noisome odor<br>一股恶臭气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers refused to enter the noisome tunnel without masks.  工人们不戴口罩不肯进入那条恶臭的隧道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obnoxious</td><td>/əbˈnɑːkʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极讨厌的，令人反感的</td><td>an obnoxious smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His obnoxious jokes ruined the whole dinner party.  他讨人厌的玩笑毁了整场晚宴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 innocuous</td><td>/ɪˈnɑːkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的；不会引起敌意的</td><td>an innocuous question<br>一个无恶意的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What seemed an innocuous question turned out to reveal her deepest secret.  看似无恶意的问题最终却揭露了她最深的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inoffensive</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈfensɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，不讨人厌的</td><td>inoffensive background music<br>不讨人厌的背景音乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is bland but inoffensive enough for family viewing.  这部电影平淡，但足够温和，适合全家观看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>repulse</b></td><td>/rɪˈpʌls/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使厌恶，排斥</td><td>repulsed by the smell<br>被那气味恶心到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sight of the rotting fruit repulsed everyone standing near the crate.  腐烂水果的样子让站在箱子旁的每个人都感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgust</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使厌恶；厌恶</td><td>disgust even his allies<br>连他的盟友也感到厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state of the kitchen disgusted the health inspector.  厨房的状况让卫生检查员十分厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nauseate</del></td><td>/ˈnɔːzieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使恶心；使厌恶</td><td>nauseate the audience<br>使观众反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The graphic descriptions in the film nauseated much of the audience.  影片中露骨的描写让大部分观众感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repel</td><td>/rɪˈpel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>击退，驱退；使厌恶</td><td>repel the attackers<br>击退进攻者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The greasy smell of the kitchen seemed to repel every customer who entered.  厨房油腻的气味似乎让每个进来的顾客都感到厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revolt</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊlt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使厌恶，使反感</td><td>revolted by the smell<br>被气味恶心到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of the spoiled milk revolted everyone in the small kitchen.  变质牛奶的气味让狭小厨房里的每个人都反胃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sicken</td><td>/ˈsɪkən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>生病，患病；使厌恶</td><td>sicken and die<br>患病死去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many of the sheep sickened after drinking from the polluted stream.  许多羊喝了污染的溪水后患病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allure</td><td>/əˈlʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引，诱惑</td><td>the allure of fame<br>名声的诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap rents allure young artists to this run-down district.  低廉的租金吸引年轻艺术家来到这个破旧街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attract</td><td>/əˈtrækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>吸引，引起</td><td>attract attention<br>吸引注意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhibition attracted thousands of visitors in its first week.  展览首周吸引了数千名参观者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bewitch</td><td>/bɪˈwɪtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷；用魔法迷住</td><td>bewitch the audience<br>让观众为之着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her singing bewitched everyone in the crowded little hall.  她的歌声让拥挤小厅里的每个人都着了迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 captivate</td><td>/ˈkæptɪveɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷，深深吸引</td><td>captivate the audience<br>深深吸引观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel captivated readers around the world.  她的第一部小说令世界各地的读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 charm</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>迷住，吸引；魅力</td><td>charm the audience<br>迷住观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist charmed the audience with her first piece.  这位年轻钢琴家用第一首曲子迷住了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enchant</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，使陶醉</td><td>be enchanted by the scenery<br>被风景迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old fishing village enchanted every visitor who stayed there.  那个古老渔村令每位住过的游客陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 entice</td><td>/ɪnˈtaɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱使，引诱</td><td>entice customers with discounts<br>用折扣吸引顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Free samples were used to entice shoppers into the new store.  免费样品被用来引诱购物者进入新店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fascinate</td><td>/ˈfæsɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈吸引，使入迷</td><td>fascinate young readers<br>使小读者着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stories about strange deep-sea creatures fascinate young readers all over the world.  关于奇异深海生物的故事让全世界的小读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lure</td><td>/ˈlʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>吸引，诱惑；诱饵</td><td>lure customers in<br>把顾客吸引进来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Low prices lured shoppers away from the older stores.  低价把购物者从老店吸引走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seduce</td><td>/sɪˈduːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱使（背离正道），引诱</td><td>seduce someone into crime<br>引诱某人犯罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 High salaries seduced many teachers away from the classroom.  高薪诱使许多教师离开了课堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tempt</td><td>/ˈtempt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱惑，引诱</td><td>tempt fate<br>冒险行事，玩火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of fresh bread tempted us into the little bakery.  新鲜面包的香味把我们诱进了那家小面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>repute</b></td><td>/rɪˈpjuːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（尤指好的）名声，名誉</td><td>a house of ill repute<br>一处声名不佳的场所</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a scholar of great repute in the field of ancient languages.  她是古代语言领域声誉卓著的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 celebrity</td><td>/səˈlebrɪti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>名人；名声</td><td>a local television celebrity<br>当地的电视名人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restaurant is full of celebrities every Friday night.  每周五晚这家餐厅都挤满名人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fame</td><td>/ˈfeɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>名声，声誉</td><td>rise to sudden fame<br>骤然成名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her fame spread quickly after the documentary aired nationwide.  纪录片在全国播出后，她的名声迅速传开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 name</td><td>/ˈneɪm/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>名声，名誉；名字</td><td>make a name for himself<br>为自己赢得名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a name for herself reporting from war zones.  她因在战区做报道而声名远播。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 renown</td><td>/rɪˈnaʊn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>声望，名誉</td><td>a chef of great renown<br>声望极高的厨师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violinist won international renown while still in her twenties.  这位小提琴家二十多岁就赢得了国际声望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reputation</td><td>/ˌrepjəˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>名声，声望</td><td>build a reputation<br>建立起声望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She built a reputation for honesty over twenty long years.  她用漫长的二十年建立起诚实的声望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 notoriety</td><td>/ˌnoʊtəˈraɪəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>坏名声</td><td>gain instant notoriety<br>立刻声名狼藉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The video gained him instant notoriety that followed him for the rest of his career.  这段视频让他立刻声名狼藉，并伴随他整个职业生涯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>requite</b></td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>酬谢，报答；报仇</td><td>requite his loyalty<br>报答他的忠诚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted to requite her kindness with a gift she would truly treasure.  他想用一件她会真正珍视的礼物报答她的善意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indemnify</td><td>/ˌɪnˈdemnəfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赔偿，补偿；保障</td><td>indemnify the owner<br>赔偿所有人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract requires the builder to indemnify us for any damage.  合同要求建筑方赔偿我们的任何损失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reciprocate</td><td>/rɪˈsɪprəkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>报答，回报</td><td>reciprocate her kindness<br>回报她的好意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They drove the neighbors to the airport to reciprocate her kindness.  他们开车送邻居去机场，以回报她的好意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recompense</td><td>/ˈrekəmˌpens/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>补偿，报酬</td><td>recompense for the loss<br>对损失的补偿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was given a small sum as recompense for her trouble.  她得到一小笔钱作为麻烦的补偿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recoup</td><td>/rɪˈkuːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>恢复，复原；补偿</td><td>recoup his strength<br>恢复体力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the long march the soldiers rested to recoup their strength.  长途行军后，士兵们休息以恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reimburse</td><td>/ˌriːɪmˈbɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偿还，报销</td><td>reimburse travel expenses<br>报销旅费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company will reimburse you for any tickets you buy.  公司会报销你购买的任何车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remunerate</td><td>/rɪˈmjuːnəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支付报酬，补偿</td><td>remunerate him generously<br>给他优厚的报酬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company promised to remunerate him generously for the extra work.  公司承诺为这些额外工作给他丰厚的报酬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repay</td><td>/riˈpeɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偿还；报答</td><td>repay a favor<br>报答一份恩情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I can never repay the patience my mentor showed me.  导师给我的耐心，我永远无法回报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avenge</td><td>/əˈvendʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>为…报仇，报复</td><td>avenge his father's death<br>为父之死报仇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He swore to avenge the insult done to his family.  他发誓要为家族所受的侮辱报仇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 redress</td><td>/rɪˈdres/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赔偿，纠正</td><td>seek legal redress<br>寻求法律赔偿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Victims went to court to seek redress for their losses.  受害者诉诸法庭为损失寻求赔偿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retaliate</td><td>/rɪˈtælieɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>报复，反击</td><td>retaliate against critics<br>报复批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 If we raise tariffs, our trading partners will almost certainly retaliate.  如果我们提高关税，贸易伙伴几乎肯定会报复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revenge</td><td>/rɪˈvendʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>报复，复仇</td><td>take revenge on<br>向…报复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waited years to take revenge on the men who ruined his family.  他等了多年才向毁掉他家庭的那些人报复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absolve</td><td>/əbˈzɑːlv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使无罪，解除责任</td><td>absolve someone of blame<br>免除某人的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report absolved the pilot of all blame.  报告免除了飞行员的全部责任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 condone</td><td>/kənˈdoʊn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宽恕；忽视</td><td>condone the violence<br>宽恕这种暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school will never condone violence between its students.  学校绝不会宽恕学生之间的暴力行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forgive</td><td>/fərˈɡɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宽容，原谅</td><td>forgive a small mistake<br>原谅小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She forgave him quickly, though the insult had stung for days.  她很快原谅了他，尽管那侮辱刺痛了她几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pardon</td><td>/ˈpɑːrdən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赦免，宽恕；原谅</td><td>grant him a pardon<br>给予他赦免</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The president pardoned two men convicted decades earlier.  总统赦免了两名几十年前被定罪的男子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>requisite</b></td><td>/ˈrekwɪzɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>必需品；必要条件</td><td>requisite skills for the job<br>工作所需的技能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Patience is a requisite for anyone hoping to teach very young children.  耐心是任何想教幼儿的人必备的条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condition</td><td>/kənˈdɪʃən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使适应，训练；条件</td><td>be conditioned to expect<br>被训练成习惯期待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Athletes are conditioned to endure long periods of pain.  运动员被训练得能长时间承受痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demand</td><td>/dɪˈmænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强烈要求；需求</td><td>demand an apology<br>要求道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The angry customers demanded an apology from the store manager.  愤怒的顾客要求店长道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 necessity</td><td>/nəˈsesəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>必需品；必要，需要</td><td>a basic necessity<br>一件基本必需品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Clean water is a necessity, not a luxury.  清洁饮水是必需品，不是奢侈品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 need</td><td>/ˈniːd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>需要，必需</td><td>need urgent help<br>需要紧急帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Plants need sunlight and water to grow properly indoors.  室内植物需要阳光和水才能正常生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 requirement</td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪrmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>要求，必要条件</td><td>meet the requirements<br>满足这些要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two years of experience is a requirement for this post.  两年经验是这个职位的必要条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compulsory</td><td>/kəmˈpʌlsəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制的，必修的</td><td>compulsory education<br>义务教育</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing a helmet is compulsory on every construction site.  在每个建筑工地戴安全帽都是强制的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperative</td><td>/ɪmˈperətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>命令的，强制性的；迫切的</td><td>an imperative need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is imperative that we repair the dam before the rains arrive.  在雨季来临前修好大坝是迫切必需的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indispensable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈspensəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可缺少的，必需的</td><td>an indispensable tool<br>一件不可缺少的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good dictionary is indispensable to any serious translator.  一本好词典对任何认真的译者都不可缺少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mandatory</td><td>/ˈmændətɔːri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制的</td><td>a mandatory requirement<br>强制性要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Safety training is mandatory for all new employees here.  这里所有新员工都必须接受安全培训。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 necessary</td><td>/ˈnesəˌseri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>必需的，必要的；必需品</td><td>a necessary expense<br>一项必要的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Clean water is necessary for even the simplest household.  即便最简朴的家庭也需要干净的水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obligatory</td><td>/əˈblɪɡətɔːri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制性的</td><td>obligatory military service<br>强制的兵役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that country obligatory military service lasts for eighteen months.  在那个国家，强制的兵役要服十八个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 required</td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>必需的，规定的</td><td>required reading<br>必读材料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A signed form is required before the trip can begin.  出行前必须提交一份签好的表格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vital</td><td>/ˈvaɪtəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满活力的；极重要的</td><td>full of vital energy<br>充满生命活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular exercise keeps older people vital and mentally alert.  经常锻炼使老年人保持活力和头脑清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 needless</td><td>/ˈniːdləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不必要的，多余的</td><td>needless worry<br>不必要的担心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay caused needless anxiety among the waiting families.  这次延误让等候的家属们平添不必要的焦虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unnecessary</td><td>/ənˈnesəˌseri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不必要的，多余的</td><td>unnecessary expense<br>不必要的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the paragraph removed an unnecessary repetition.  删掉这段去掉了一处不必要的重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rescind</b></td><td>/rɪˈsɪnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>废除，取消</td><td>rescind an offer<br>撤回报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company rescinded the job offer without explanation.  公司未加解释便撤回了聘用通知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abolish</td><td>/əˈbɑːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>废除，取消</td><td>abolish the death penalty<br>废除死刑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government promised to abolish the unpopular entrance fee.  政府承诺废除那项不受欢迎的入场费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrogate</td><td>/ˈæbrəɡeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>正式废除（法律、条约）</td><td>abrogate a treaty<br>废除条约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parliament voted to abrogate the outdated law.  议会投票废除这项过时的法律。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annul</td><td>/əˈnʌl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣告无效，取消</td><td>annul the marriage<br>宣告婚姻无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court agreed to annul the marriage after only three weeks.  法院同意在仅仅三周之后宣告这段婚姻无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cancel</td><td>/ˈkænsəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>取消，撤销；抵消</td><td>cancel the meeting<br>取消会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They canceled the outdoor concert because of the heavy rain.  因为大雨，他们取消了露天音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invalidate</td><td>/ɪnˈvælɪdeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使无效，使作废</td><td>invalidate a contract<br>使合同无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A missing signature will invalidate the whole form.  缺少签名会使整张表格失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negate</td><td>/nɪˈɡeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；否定，取消</td><td>negate the effect<br>抵消效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless error can negate weeks of careful work.  一个粗心的错误能抹掉数周的细致工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 null</td><td>/ˈnʌl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的；等于零的</td><td>null and void<br>无效的，作废的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court declared the agreement null and void.  法院宣布该协议无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nullify</td><td>/ˈnʌləfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使无效，废除</td><td>nullify an agreement<br>使协议失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge nullified the election result.  法官宣布选举结果无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recall</td><td>/rɪˈkɔːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>撤销，召回；回忆</td><td>recall the ambassador<br>召回大使</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The maker recalled thousands of cars with faulty brakes.  制造商召回了数千辆刹车有缺陷的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repeal</td><td>/rɪˈpiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除（法律等）</td><td>repeal the old law<br>废除旧法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lawmakers voted to repeal the outdated statute that had confused courts for decades.  议员们投票废除了这部困扰法院数十年的过时法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revoke</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除；吊销</td><td>revoke a license<br>吊销执照</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court revoked his driving license after the second offense.  法院在他第二次违法后吊销了他的驾照。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 void</td><td>/ˈvɔɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词、名词</td><td>使无效；排空；空虚</td><td>void the contract<br>使合同无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Missing a single payment can void the entire warranty agreement.  漏付一期就可能使整个保修协议失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 establish</td><td>/ɪˈstæblɪʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>确证，证实；建立</td><td>establish the facts beyond doubt<br>确证事实无疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The investigation established the facts beyond any reasonable doubt.  调查毫无疑问地确证了事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enact</td><td>/ɪˈnækt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>制定或颁布（法律）</td><td>enact a new tax law<br>制定新税法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parliament moved quickly to enact a new tax law before the recess.  议会在休会前迅速制定了一部新税法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reserved</b></td><td>/rɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内向的，缄默的</td><td>a quiet, reserved man<br>安静内向的男子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was a reserved man who rarely spoke about his feelings.  她的父亲是个内向的人，很少谈论自己的感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 closemouthed</del></td><td>/ˈkloʊsˈmaʊðd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默寡言的，不透露的</td><td>closemouthed about details<br>对细节守口如瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials stayed closemouthed about the ongoing negotiations.  官员们对正在进行的谈判守口如瓶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constrained</td><td>/kənˈstreɪnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨的，不自然的；受限的</td><td>a constrained smile<br>一个勉强的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their conversation was polite but oddly constrained.  他们的谈话礼貌，却出奇地拘谨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laconic</td><td>/ləˈkɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁（以至于显得粗鲁或难以理解）的</td><td>a laconic reply<br>简短生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laconic reply told us nothing about what had actually happened.  他简短生硬的回答没有告诉我们实际发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reticent</td><td>/ˈretɪsnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默不语的</td><td>reticent about his past<br>对往事讳莫如深</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was oddly reticent about the years he spent working abroad.  他对自己在国外工作的那些年出奇地沉默不语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restrained</td><td>/rɪˈstreɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>受约束的，克制的</td><td>restrained by rules<br>受规则约束</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His restrained reply hid how angry he actually felt inside.  他克制的回答掩盖了他内心真正的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 silent</td><td>/ˈsaɪlənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默的，无声的</td><td>remain silent<br>保持沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained silent throughout the entire family argument.  整场家庭争执中他一直沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 taciturn</td><td>/ˈtæsɪtɜːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默寡言的，话少的</td><td>a taciturn farmer<br>沉默寡言的农夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The taciturn farmer answered our questions with a nod or a shrug.  沉默寡言的农夫只用点头或耸肩回答我们的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 communicative</td><td>/kəˈmjuːnɪkətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，愿意交流的</td><td>a communicative host<br>健谈的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew more communicative after the second cup of strong tea.  喝下第二杯浓茶后他变得更爱说话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expansive</td><td>/ɪkˈspænsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>话多的，健谈的</td><td>in an expansive mood<br>谈兴正浓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After dinner he grew expansive and told stories all evening.  晚饭后他变得健谈，整晚讲故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 talkative</td><td>/ˈtɔːkətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，爱说话的</td><td>a talkative passenger<br>健谈的乘客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our talkative driver described every building along the winding coastal road.  健谈的司机介绍了蜿蜒海滨路上的每座建筑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>residue</b></td><td>/ˈrezɪduː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>剩余物</td><td>a sticky residue<br>黏稠的残留物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dark residue remained at the bottom of the flask.  烧瓶底部留下了深色的残留物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debris</td><td>/dəˈbriː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>碎片，残骸</td><td>clear away the debris<br>清理废墟碎片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rescuers dug through the debris for hours looking for survivors.  救援人员在废墟中挖掘了数小时，寻找生还者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remainder</td><td>/rɪˈmeɪndər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>剩余部分，余数</td><td>the remainder of the day<br>这一天余下的时间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent the remainder of the money on books.  我们把剩下的钱都花在买书上了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remnant</td><td>/ˈremnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>残余，剩余物</td><td>the last remnants of<br>…最后的残余</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a few remnants of the ancient wall still stand today.  今天只剩下古城墙的少量残余仍然矗立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>resign</b></td><td>/rɪˈzaɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辞职，放弃（职位）</td><td>resign from office<br>辞去公职</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister resigned from office after the scandal broke.  丑闻爆发后，那位部长辞职了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abnegate</del></td><td>/ˈæbnɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>放弃，否认</td><td>abnegate one's rights<br>放弃自己的权利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monk chose to abnegate all personal comforts.  这位僧人选择放弃一切个人享受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cede</td><td>/siːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放弃，割让</td><td>cede territory<br>割让领土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The defeated nation was forced to cede two provinces.  战败国被迫割让两个省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relinquish</td><td>/rɪˈlɪŋkwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放弃（权力、职位）</td><td>relinquish control<br>放弃控制权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to relinquish control of the family business.  她拒绝放弃对家族企业的控制权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 renounce</td><td>/rɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>正式放弃，声明弃绝</td><td>renounce one's claim<br>声明放弃权利主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He renounced his claim to the inheritance in writing.  他以书面形式放弃了继承权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surrender</td><td>/səˈrendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>交出，放弃，投降</td><td>surrender to the police<br>向警方投降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief finally surrendered to the police after a long chase.  经过长时间追捕，小偷终于向警方投降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 step down</td><td>/step ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辞职，下台</td><td>step down as chairman<br>辞去主席职务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He will step down as chairman at the end of the year.  他将于年底辞去董事长职务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assume</td><td>/əˈsuːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>就任，承担（职权）；假定</td><td>assume office<br>就职上任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She will assume office as director next Monday.  她将于下周一就任主任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inaugurate</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːɡjəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>开始</td><td>inaugurate a new era<br>开创一个新时代</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty inaugurated a long period of peace between the two nations.  这项条约开启了两国之间长期的和平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>resilience</b></td><td>/rɪˈzɪliəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恢复能力</td><td>remarkable resilience<br>非凡的恢复力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed remarkable resilience after losing everything in the fire.  在火灾中失去一切后，她表现出惊人的恢复力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adaptability</td><td>/əˌdæptəˈbɪləti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>适应能力，可变性</td><td>great adaptability<br>很强的适应能力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The species survives through sheer adaptability to new habitats.  这个物种全靠对新栖息地的适应力而存活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>resolute</b></td><td>/ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的</td><td>remain resolute<br>保持坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite heavy criticism, the mayor remained resolute about closing the old factory.  尽管备受批评，市长在关闭旧工厂一事上依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bound</td><td>/baʊnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>界限；决心的，一定会的</td><td>within the bounds of law<br>在法律范围内</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her curiosity knew no bounds when she entered the old library.  她走进那座旧图书馆时，好奇心毫无边界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decided</td><td>/dɪˈsaɪdɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚决的，明确的</td><td>a decided advantage<br>明显的优势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a decided tone that ended all discussion.  她用坚决的语气说话，结束了所有讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faithful</td><td>/ˈfeɪθfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠实的；如实的</td><td>a faithful translation<br>忠实的译文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is a faithful adaptation of the original novel.  这部电影是对原著小说的忠实改编。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resolved</td><td>/riˈzɑːlvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>下定决心的，坚决的</td><td>resolved to continue<br>决心继续下去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was resolved to finish the book before the winter ended.  他决心在冬天结束前写完这本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwavering</td><td>/ənˈweɪvərɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定不移的，不动摇的</td><td>unwavering support<br>坚定不移的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unwavering belief in the plan convinced the whole committee.  她对方案坚定不移的信心说服了整个委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faltering</td><td>/ˈfɔːltərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫的；蹒跚的</td><td>a faltering voice<br>犹豫颤抖的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered in a faltering voice, unsure of herself.  她声音犹豫地回答，显得没有把握。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hesitant</td><td>/ˈhezɪtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫的，迟疑的</td><td>hesitant about the offer<br>对这个提议犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was hesitant about moving to a new city alone.  他对独自搬到新城市感到犹豫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indecisive</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈsaɪsɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫不决的；非决定性的</td><td>hopelessly indecisive<br>极其优柔寡断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is too indecisive to lead the team.  他太犹豫不决，无法带领团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 irresolute</del></td><td>/ɪˈrezəluːt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫不决的</td><td>an irresolute leader<br>优柔寡断的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The irresolute committee postponed the decision for another month.  犹豫不决的委员会又把决定推迟了一个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jittery</td><td>/ˈdʒɪtəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧张不安的，神经紧张的</td><td>jittery investors<br>紧张不安的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too much coffee left him jittery before the interview.  咖啡喝太多让他面试前紧张不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vacillating</td><td>/ˈvæsəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>摇摆不定的，犹豫的</td><td>a vacillating leader<br>摇摆不定的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company suffered under a vacillating chief executive.  公司在一位摇摆不定的总裁手下吃尽苦头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wavering</td><td>/ˈweɪvərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动摇的，游移不定的</td><td>wavering support<br>动摇的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Polls showed the wavering support of rural voters.  民调显示农村选民的支持正在动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>resonant</b></td><td>/ˈrezənənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）洪亮的，共鸣的</td><td>a resonant voice<br>一个洪亮的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His resonant voice filled the stone hall without any need for a microphone.  他洪亮的嗓音无需麦克风便充满了石砌大厅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consonant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsənənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和谐一致的</td><td>consonant with tradition<br>与传统相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His modest lifestyle is consonant with the values he preaches.  他朴素的生活方式与他宣扬的价值观相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 orotund</del></td><td>/ˈɔrəˌtʌnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）洪亮的；夸张浮华的</td><td>an orotund delivery<br>浑厚夸张的演讲腔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's orotund phrases impressed nobody in the small room.  参议员浮夸洪亮的措辞在这小房间里没打动任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 plangent</del></td><td>/ˈplændʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）响亮而哀伤的</td><td>a plangent cry<br>哀伤的呼声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plangent cry of a gull echoed across the empty beach.  海鸥凄凉的叫声在空荡的海滩上回响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resounding</td><td>/rɪˈzaʊndɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>响亮的，回响的；巨大的</td><td>a resounding crash<br>响亮的撞击声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The door shut with a resounding bang behind him.  门在他身后砰然作响地关上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reverberant</td><td>/rɪˈvɜrbərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>回响的，反射声音的</td><td>a reverberant hall<br>一个回声很大的大厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their footsteps echoed in the reverberant stone corridor.  他们的脚步声在回声很大的石廊里回荡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sonorous</td><td>/ˈsɑːnərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音洪亮的</td><td>a sonorous bell<br>一口洪亮的钟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The priest read the passage in a slow, sonorous voice.  神父用缓慢洪亮的声音朗读那段经文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vibrant</td><td>/ˈvaɪbrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满活力的；鲜艳的</td><td>a vibrant city<br>一座充满活力的城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighborhood became a vibrant hub for young artists.  这个街区成了年轻艺术家的活力中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faint</td><td>/ˈfeɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；微弱的</td><td>a faint voice<br>微弱的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt faint after standing in the sun too long.  在太阳下站太久后她感到虚弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 low</td><td>/ˈloʊ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>低落的，沮丧的；低的</td><td>feel a bit low<br>感觉有点低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has been feeling low since her closest friend moved away.  自从最好的朋友搬走，她一直情绪低落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 muffled</td><td>/ˈmʌfəld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>压低的，模糊不清的</td><td>muffled voices<br>低沉模糊的说话声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We heard muffled laughter through the closed door.  我们透过关着的门听到低沉的笑声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 muted</td><td>/ˈmjuːtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>低调的，柔和的；压低的</td><td>a muted response<br>反应平淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The announcement drew a surprisingly muted response from investors.  这项公告得到投资者出奇平淡的反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smothered</td><td>/ˈsmʌðərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>压低的，被压抑的</td><td>a smothered cry<br>一声被压住的叫喊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a smothered sob and turned toward the wall.  她压着抽泣了一声，转向墙壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soft</td><td>/ˈsɑːft/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>柔和的，轻微的</td><td>a soft voice<br>轻柔的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a soft tone so the baby would not wake.  她轻声说话，以免把婴儿弄醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weak</td><td>/ˈwiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>脆弱的，虚弱的</td><td>a weak structure<br>脆弱的结构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge has a weak structure and cannot bear trucks.  这座桥结构脆弱，无法承载卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>resourceful</b></td><td>/rɪˈsɔːrsfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有创造力的，机智的</td><td>a resourceful leader<br>一位机智的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A resourceful traveler can find shelter even in an unfamiliar mountain village.  机智的旅行者即使在陌生的山村也能找到住处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 able</td><td>/ˈeɪbəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有能力的，能干的</td><td>an able administrator<br>一位能干的管理者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He proved an unusually able negotiator during the crisis.  危机中他显示出非凡的谈判能力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adroit</td><td>/əˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，灵巧的</td><td>adroit at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She proved remarkably adroit at handling hostile questions from reporters.  她在应对记者的敌意提问时表现得非常娴熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 competent</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpətɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有能力的，胜任的</td><td>a competent surgeon<br>称职的外科医生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was written by a competent but uninspired analyst.  报告出自一位称职却缺乏灵气的分析师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 talented</td><td>/ˈtæləntɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有才华的，天资高的</td><td>a talented young pianist<br>一位有才华的年轻钢琴家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hires talented designers straight out of school.  这家公司直接从学校招聘有才华的设计师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>respite</b></td><td>/ˈrespɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>间歇，休息</td><td>a brief respite<br>短暂的休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The afternoon rain gave the firefighters a brief respite from the intense heat.  午后的雨让消防员从酷热中得到短暂的休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 break</td><td>/ˈbreɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>损坏，打破；间歇</td><td>break the vase<br>打破花瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat broke the vase while chasing a moth indoors.  猫在屋里追蛾子时打破了花瓶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lull</td><td>/lʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>相对平静时期，间隙；使镇静，使安心</td><td>a lull in the fighting<br>战斗中的间隙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lull in the fighting allowed medics to reach the wounded.  战斗中短暂的间隙让医护人员得以接近伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intermission</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>中场休息，间歇</td><td>during the intermission<br>在中场休息时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We bought coffee during the long intermission.  漫长的中场休息时我们买了咖啡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recess</td><td>/rɪˈses/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>休会，休庭；课间休息</td><td>take a short recess<br>短暂休会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee will recess for lunch and resume at two.  委员会将休会吃午饭，两点复会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pause</td><td>/ˈpɔːz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暂停，间歇</td><td>a brief pause<br>短暂的停顿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After a long pause she finally answered the question.  长久的停顿之后，她终于回答了问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rest</td><td>/ˈrest/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>休息，静止；剩余部分</td><td>take a short rest<br>稍作休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor ordered him to rest for at least a week.  医生要他至少休息一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resumption</td><td>/rɪˈzʌmpʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恢复，重新开始</td><td>the resumption of talks<br>谈判的恢复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone welcomed the resumption of the peace talks.  各方都欢迎和谈的恢复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>retroactive</b></td><td>/ˌretroʊˈæktɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有追溯效力的</td><td>a retroactive pay raise<br>可追溯生效的加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new rule is retroactive to the first of January.  这项新规定可追溯至一月一日生效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>resplendent</b></td><td>/rɪˈsplendənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>华丽辉煌的</td><td>resplendent in uniform<br>身着华丽制服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bride appeared resplendent in a gown embroidered with hundreds of tiny pearls.  新娘身着绣满数百颗小珍珠的礼服，光彩夺目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brilliant</td><td>/ˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪颖的；辉煌的</td><td>a brilliant student<br>才华出众的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a brilliant mathematician long before she turned twenty.  她还没到二十岁就已是出色的数学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorious</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辉煌的，壮丽的</td><td>a glorious summer sunset<br>壮丽的夏日落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountains looked glorious in the late afternoon light.  傍晚的光线下群山显得壮丽辉煌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gorgeous</td><td>/ˈɡɔːrdʒəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，绚丽的</td><td>a gorgeous sunset<br>绚丽的落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We watched a gorgeous sunset from the hotel balcony.  我们在酒店阳台上看了一场绚丽的落日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnificent</td><td>/mæɡˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的</td><td>a magnificent view<br>壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we enjoyed a magnificent view of the whole valley.  从山顶我们欣赏到整个山谷的壮丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendid</td><td>/ˈsplendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的；极好的</td><td>a splendid marble hall<br>壮丽的大理石厅堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the roof we had a splendid view of the harbor.  从屋顶望去，港口的景色极为壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sublime</td><td>/səˈblaɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；壮丽的；卓越的</td><td>a sublime landscape<br>崇高壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we watched a sublime landscape unfold below us.  从山顶我们看着一片壮丽的景色在下方展开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superb</td><td>/sʊˈpɜːrb/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极好的，卓越的</td><td>a superb performance<br>一场卓越的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young violinist gave a superb performance that night.  那晚这位年轻小提琴手献上了卓越的表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dim</td><td>/ˈdɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>暗淡的，模糊的</td><td>a dim light<br>昏暗的灯光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was too dim for reading anything but large print.  房间太暗，只能看大字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lackluster</td><td>/ˈlæklʌstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>黯淡无光泽的</td><td>a lackluster performance<br>平淡无光的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lackluster hair and tired face worried everyone at home.  她黯淡的头发和疲惫的脸让家人都担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>responsive</b></td><td>/rɪˈspɑːnsɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>反应积极的；敏感的</td><td>responsive to criticism<br>对批评有回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new manager is unusually responsive to suggestions from the factory floor.  这位新经理对车间提出的建议反应格外积极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prompt</td><td>/ˈprɑːmpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>促使，引起；提示</td><td>prompt an inquiry<br>引发一场调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaked memo prompted an immediate inquiry by regulators.  泄露的备忘录促使监管机构立即展开调查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sensible</td><td>/ˈsensəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，合理的</td><td>a sensible choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing boots was a sensible choice in that mud.  在那种泥地里穿靴子是明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sensitive</td><td>/ˈsensətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏感的，灵敏的</td><td>sensitive to light<br>对光敏感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This instrument is sensitive enough to detect the faintest vibration.  这台仪器灵敏到能测出最微弱的震动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 susceptible</td><td>/səˈseptəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易受影响的；易感染的</td><td>susceptible to infection<br>易受感染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young children are especially susceptible to infection during the cold months.  幼儿在寒冷的月份里尤其容易受到感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dispassionate</td><td>/dɪsˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静客观的，不带感情的</td><td>a dispassionate analysis<br>冷静客观的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge gave a dispassionate summary of both arguments.  法官冷静客观地总结了双方的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>restive</b></td><td>/ˈrestɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦躁不安的；不驯服的</td><td>a restive crowd<br>焦躁不安的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience became restive when the concert started forty minutes late.  音乐会迟了四十分钟才开始，观众变得焦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restless</td><td>/ˈrestləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦躁不安的；不平静的</td><td>a restless night<br>不安宁的一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a restless night listening to the wind rattle the windows.  他度过了一个不平静的夜晚，听着风摇窗户作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nervy</td><td>/ˈnɜːrvi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧张不安的；大胆无礼的</td><td>feel nervy<br>感到紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse grew nervy as the crowd pressed closer.  人群靠近时，那匹马变得紧张不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uneasy</td><td>/əˈniːzi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不安的，忧虑的</td><td>an uneasy silence<br>一阵令人不安的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt uneasy about leaving the children alone upstairs.  把孩子单独留在楼上让她感到不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uptight</td><td>/əpˈtaɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧张的，拘谨的</td><td>get uptight about<br>为…紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't get so uptight about a single bad grade.  别为一次成绩不好这么紧张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperturbable</td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈtɜːrbəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的；不慌不忙的</td><td>imperturbable calm<br>泰然自若的镇定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot stayed imperturbable while the engine warning sounded.  发动机警报响起时，飞行员依然沉着冷静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>restless</b></td><td>/ˈrestləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦躁不安的；不平静的</td><td>a restless night<br>不安宁的一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a restless night listening to the wind rattle the windows.  他度过了一个不平静的夜晚，听着风摇窗户作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agitating</td><td>/ˈædʒəˌteɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不安的，扰乱的</td><td>an agitating rumor<br>一个令人不安的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agitating news kept everyone awake until morning.  这令人不安的消息让大家一直醒到早上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anxious</td><td>/ˈæŋkʃəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦虑的，担心的</td><td>anxious about the results<br>为结果焦虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew anxious as the deadline drew closer.  截止日临近，她越来越焦虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 distressful</del></td><td>/dɪˈstrɛsfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人痛苦的，使人难受的</td><td>a distressful memory<br>一段痛苦的回忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Testifying about the accident was distressful for the young witness.  就事故作证对这名年轻证人来说十分痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbing</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不安的，扰人的</td><td>a disturbing report<br>一份令人不安的报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film contains several disturbing scenes of urban poverty.  这部影片有几处描写城市贫困的令人不安的场景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fraught</td><td>/frɔːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满（危险）的；忧虑的</td><td>fraught with danger<br>充满危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountain route is fraught with danger in early spring.  早春时节这条山路危机四伏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restive</td><td>/ˈrestɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦躁不安的；不驯服的</td><td>a restive crowd<br>焦躁不安的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience became restive when the concert started forty minutes late.  音乐会迟了四十分钟才开始，观众变得焦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perturbed</td><td>/pərˈtɜːrbd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦虑的，心烦意乱的</td><td>perturbed by the delay<br>因延误而心烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed perturbed by the letter she had just opened.  她刚打开的那封信似乎让她心烦意乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uneasy</td><td>/əˈniːzi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不安的，忧虑的</td><td>an uneasy silence<br>一阵令人不安的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt uneasy about leaving the children alone upstairs.  把孩子单独留在楼上让她感到不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unquiet</td><td>/ʌnˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平静的，不安的</td><td>an unquiet mind<br>不安的心绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent an unquiet night waiting for the test results.  他不安地度过一夜，等着检测结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsettled</td><td>/ʌnˈsetəld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>动荡的，多变的；未定的</td><td>unsettled weather<br>多变的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unsettled weather ruined all our plans for the weekend.  多变的天气打乱了我们全部周末计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 halcyon</td><td>/ˈhælsiən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宁静的，平静的</td><td>halcyon days<br>宁静美好的日子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He often talked about the halcyon days of his youth.  他常谈起年轻时那些宁静美好的日子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peaceful</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和平的，平静的</td><td>a peaceful settlement<br>和平的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally chose a peaceful path out of conflict.  双方最终选择了走出冲突的和平道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serene</td><td>/səˈriːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，安详的</td><td>a serene smile<br>安详的微笑</td></tr>
